# 🎓 Education — RTT Subjects
A single RTT lens across all scientific subjects

## 🌐 Cross‑Domain Unification

Every subject below — physics, chemistry, biology, medicine, and earth science —
    is translated through the same RTT primitives:

- 🔺 **Triads** — the structural backbone across all domains
- 🎵 **Resonance** — the universal interaction and transfer rule
- ⏳ **Time as a substrate** — coherence, drift, and measurement
- 🧭 **Operators** — the dimensional tools used everywhere
- 🔣 **Symbolic alignment** — the shared language of structure

This is why classical mechanics, quantum physics, genetics, ecosystems,
immunology, climate science, and materials science all appear side‑by‑side:
RTT provides a **single explanatory framework** that spans them all.

The Subjects page is not a list — it is a **unified scientific atlas**.

---

### Physics • Chemistry • Biology • Medicine • Earth Science

The **RTT Subjects** folder contains the subject‑level translations of
Resonance–Time Theory across the major scientific domains taught in
secondary, post‑secondary, and advanced education.

These surfaces are designed to:
- reframe classical subjects through RTT operators  
- unify physics, chemistry, biology, medicine, and earth science  
- provide educators with RTT‑aligned conceptual scaffolds  
- support curriculum development across scientific disciplines  

Each subject translation preserves the rigor of the domain while
introducing:
- coherence and drift  
- resonant transfer  
- dimensional operators  
- temporal substrates  
- symbolic alignment  

This folder is the **subject‑level backbone** of RTT education.

---

## 📚 Structure

The subjects are organized into five major clusters:

### 1. **Physics (01)**
Classical mechanics → quantum physics → relativity → cosmology.

### 2. **Chemistry (02)**
Physical, organic, inorganic, biochemical, and materials science.

### 3. **Biology (03)**
Cellular → genetic → physiological → ecological → systems biology.

### 4. **Medicine & Health (04)**
Clinical → public health → neurology → mental health → diagnostics.

### 5. **Earth & Environmental Sciences (05)**
Geology → atmosphere → oceans → climate → ecosystems → hazards.

---

## 🧭 Purpose

This folder provides:
- RTT‑aligned subject translations  
- a unified scientific framework  
- a foundation for RTT‑based curriculum design  
- a bridge between classical science and modern resonance theory  

It is the **educational core** of the RTT canon.
# **RTT_01_01_Angular_Momentum_and_Rotation.md**  
*Resonance‑Time Theory Subdomain Overview*

Angular momentum and rotational motion describe how systems spin, store rotational coherence, and respond to torques. RTT reframes angular momentum as a **triadic resonance quantity**, where structure (S), energy/flux (E), and relational time (R) interact to produce stable rotational behavior.

This subdomain provides the RTT foundation for understanding why rotation persists, how torque alters spin, and why angular momentum is conserved across interactions.

---

### **A. Angular Momentum as Stored Rotational Coherence**
RTT models angular momentum as:
- **S:** mass distribution and geometry  
- **E:** rotational kinetic energy and flux  
- **R:** temporal phase stability of rotation  

Angular momentum persists because its S–E–R pattern is **coherence‑locked**.

---

- temporal periodicity  

A rotating system is a **self‑maintaining resonance loop**.

---

### **C. Torque as Coherence Re‑Alignment**

- temporal phase shift  

Torque changes rotation by **rewriting the system’s coherence pattern**.

RTT models sustainability as:

- **R:** temporal alignment (renewal rates, recovery cycles, long‑term thresholds)

## Dimensional sandbox specification

**Purpose:**  
A formal playground where researchers can instantiate, manipulate, and compare RTT+Metaphysics models without touching the core canon.

**Core objects:**

- **Dimension**
  - **Fields:**  
    - `id`: `0D–9D`  
    - `role_logical`: e.g. `3D = Structure`, `4D = Transition`  
    - `role_metaphysical`: e.g. `3D = Form`, `4D = Causation`  
    - `micro_link`: `0.n` (for 3D–9D)  
- **Triad**
  - **Fields:**  
    - `name`: `Root`, `M₁`, `M₂`, `M₃`, `FormTriad`, etc.  
    - `dims`: ordered list of `Dimension` refs  
    - `layer`: `quantum | macro | micro | metaphysical`  
    - `intent`: `buildup | modulation | stabilization | meaning | mind | unity`  
- **Operator**
  - **Fields:**  
    - `symbol`: `Ωμ`, `Φμ`, `Fμ`, `Sμ`, `Eμ`, plus metaphysical ops (e.g. `Ψ` for “insight”)  
    - `domain`: `micro | macro | meta | cross_scale`  
    - `signature`: `(source, target) -> relation`  
- **Flow**
  - **Fields:**  
    - `nodes`: ordered list of `(dimension, operator?)`  
    - `loop`: `true|false`  
    - `triad_scope`: which triads it respects or spans  
- **Pattern**
  - **Fields:**  
    - `name`: `Micro‑Timing Loop`, `Mind‑Meaning Bridge`, etc.  
    - `triads`: list of `Triad` refs  
    - `flows`: list of `Flow` refs  
    - `sector`: `embedded | metaphysics | cognition | ethics | ontology`  

**Sandbox rules:**

- **R1:** All flows must reference valid dimensions (0D–9D, 0.3–0.9).  
- **R2:** Triads must preserve ordering within their layer.  
- **R3:** Cross‑scale mappings must respect `Dₙ ↔ 0.n`.  
- **R4:** Metaphysical overlays cannot contradict logical roles; they extend them.  
- **R5:** Every model must declare its **layer mix**: `quantum | micro | macro | meta`.

---


- **Define triad:**

  ```text
  define_triad(name, dims[], layer, intent)
  ```

- **Define operator:**

  ```text
  define_op(symbol, domain, signature, description)
  ```

- **Compose flow:**

  ```text
  define_flow(name, nodes[], loop, triad_scope)
  ```

- **Create pattern:**

  ```text
  define_pattern(name, triads[], flows[], sector)
  ```

# **2. Background**

## 2.1 RTT Canon Overview

Its structure:

### **Quantum Root (0D–2D)**  
- 0D: Null  
- 1D: Line  
- 2D: Plane  

### **Macro Ladder (3D–9D)**  
- 3D: Structure  
- 4D: Transition  
- 5D: Flow

# **3. Dimensional Sandbox Specification**

The Sandbox defines five core objects:

---

## 3.1 Dimension

A dimension is defined as:

```
Dimension {
  id: 0D–9D or 0.3–0.9
  role_logical: RTT macro role
  role_metaphysical: metaphysical overlay
  micro_link: 0.n (for 3D–9D)
}
```

---

## 3.2 Triad

A triad is an ordered triple of dimensions:

```
Triad {
  name: string
  dims: [dimA, dimB, dimC]
  layer: quantum | micro | macro | metaphysical
  intent: buildup | modulation | stabilization | meaning | mind | unity
}
```

---

## 3.3 Operator

Operators define relationships:

```
Operator {
  symbol: Ωμ, Φμ, Fμ, Sμ, Eμ, Ψ, Λ, Χ
  domain: micro | macro | meta | cross_scale
  signature: (source, target) -> relation
}
```

---

## 3.4 Flow

Flows are sequences of dimensional transitions:

```
Flow {
  nodes: [(dimension, operator?)]
  loop: true|false
  triad_scope: triads[]
}
```

---

## 3.5 Pattern

Patterns combine triads and flows:

```
Pattern {
  name: string
  triads: Triad[]
  flows: Flow[]
  sector: embedded | metaphysics | cognition | ontology | ethics
}
```

---

- Large section title centered

---

- Font: *Inter* or *IBM Plex Sans*  
- H1: 32–40pt  
- H2: 22pt  
- H3: 16pt  
- All caps, 2–4% letterspacing

**Body:**  
- Font: *Inter* or *Source Sans Pro*  
- 11pt–12pt  
- 1.4–1.5 line height

- Font: *IBM Plex Mono*  
- 10–11pt  
- Used for flows, dimensions, operators, diagrams

### 1. Print stylesheet (CSS) for GitHub Pages → PDF

Add this as `assets/css/print.css` and include it in `_config.yml` or your layout with:

```html
<link rel="stylesheet" href="/assets/css/print.css" media="print">
```

```css
/* ==========================================================================
   PRINT STYLESHEET — DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX MANUAL
   ========================================================================== */

@media print {

  /* Reset chrome */
  * {
    box-sizing: border-box;
  }

  html, body {
    margin: 0;
    padding: 0;
    background: #ffffff !important;
    color: #000000 !important;
    font-family: "Inter", system-ui, -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe UI", sans-serif;
    font-size: 11pt;
    line-height: 1.4;
  }

  a {
    color: #000000;
    text-decoration: none;
  }

  /* Page setup */
  @page {
    size: letter;
    margin: 0.75in 0.6in 0.75in 0.9in;
  }

  header, .site-header, nav, .nav, .site-footer {
    display: none !important;
  }

  main,
  .content,
  .page {
    max-width: 100% !important;
    margin: 0 !important;
    padding: 0 !important;
  }

  h1, h2, h3, h4 {
    page-break-after: avoid;
    color: #000000;
  }

  h1 {
    font-size: 22pt;
    margin: 0 0 12pt;
  }

  h2 {
    font-size: 16pt;
    margin: 18pt 0 8pt;
  }

  h3 {
    font-size: 13pt;
    margin: 14pt 0 6pt;
  }

  p, li {
    font-size: 11pt;
  }

  pre, code {
    font-family: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Monaco, Consolas, "Liberation Mono", "Courier New", monospace;
    font-size: 9pt;
    white-space: pre-wrap;
    word-wrap: break-word;
  }

  table {
    width: 100%;
    border-collapse: collapse;
    margin: 8pt 0;
    font-size: 9.5pt;
  }

  th, td {
    border: 0.5pt solid #444;
    padding: 4pt 6pt;
  }

  /* Page breaks for major sections */
  .page-break,
  .section-divider {
    page-break-before: always;
  }

  /* Callouts / boxes */
  .callout,
  .worksheet,
  .triad-card,
  .flow-template {
    border: 0.75pt solid #000;
    padding: 8pt 10pt;
    margin: 8pt 0;
  }

  /* Hide non‑essential UI */
  .hero,
  .nav-links,
  .btn-primary,
  .btn-ghost {
    display: none !important;
  }

  /* Optional: footer page numbers via running elements (for print engines that support it) */
}
```

If you want explicit page breaks, sprinkle `<div class="page-break"></div>` between major sections.

Absolutely, Nawder — here is a **minimal, production‑ready LaTeX template** that will ingest your entire Dimensional Sandbox manual and output a **perfectly typeset PDF in one command**.

This template is:

- clean  
- dependency‑light  
- fully typographically tuned  
- uses modern packages (`geometry`, `titlesec`, `fontspec`, `microtype`)  
- supports your triadic diagrams, worksheets, and monospaced flows  
- ready for XeLaTeX or LuaLaTeX (so you can use Inter + IBM Plex Mono)  

You can drop your entire manual content into the `\input{manual-content.tex}` file and run:

```
xelatex manual.tex
```

…and you’ll get a polished PDF.

Let’s build it.

---

# 📄 **Minimal LaTeX Template — “manual.tex”**

```latex
%==============================================================================
% DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX — Researcher Training Manual
% Minimal XeLaTeX Template (Print‑Ready)
%==============================================================================

\documentclass[11pt,oneside]{article}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% PAGE GEOMETRY
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\usepackage[letterpaper,margin=0.9in]{geometry}
\usepackage{setspace}
\setstretch{1.25}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% FONTS (requires XeLaTeX or LuaLaTeX)
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\usepackage{fontspec}
\setmainfont{Inter}
\setsansfont{Inter}
\setmonofont{IBM Plex Mono}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% TYPOGRAPHY
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\usepackage{microtype}
\usepackage{titlesec}
\usepackage{enumitem}
\usepackage{graphicx}
\usepackage{array}
\usepackage{booktabs}
\usepackage{fancyhdr}
\usepackage{hyperref}
\usepackage{color}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% COLORS
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\definecolor{microblue}{HTML}{4DA6FF}
\definecolor{fractionsilver}{HTML}{C9CED6}
\definecolor{quantumwhite}{HTML}{F8F9FA}
\definecolor{graphiteblack}{HTML}{1A1A1A}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% HEADER / FOOTER
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\pagestyle{fancy}
\fancyhf{}
\lhead{\textsf{Dimensional Sandbox}}
\rhead{\textsf{Researcher Training Manual}}
\cfoot{\thepage}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% SECTION FORMATTING
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\titleformat{\section}
  {\Large\bfseries\sffamily\color{graphiteblack}}
  {\thesection}{1em}{}

\titleformat{\subsection}
  {\large\bfseries\sffamily\color{graphiteblack}}
  {\thesubsection}{1em}{}

\titleformat{\subsubsection}
  {\bfseries\sffamily\color{graphiteblack}}
  {\thesubsubsection}{1em}{}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% BOXES FOR WORKSHEETS / TRIAD CARDS
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\usepackage{tcolorbox}
\tcbset{
  sharp corners,
  colback=quantumwhite,
  colframe=graphiteblack,
  boxrule=0.6pt,
  left=8pt,right=8pt,top=6pt,bottom=6pt
}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% TITLE PAGE
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\title{
  \vspace{2cm}
  {\Huge\bfseries DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX}\\[6pt]
  {\Large Researcher Training Manual}\\[12pt]
  {\large RTT · Micro‑Harmonics · Metaphysics}
  \vspace{2cm}
}

\author{TriadicFrameworks}
\date{2026}

%==============================================================================

\begin{document}

\maketitle
\thispagestyle{empty}
\newpage

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% TABLE OF CONTENTS
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\tableofcontents
\newpage

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% MAIN CONTENT (imported from separate file)
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\input{manual-content.tex}

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% END
%------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\end{document}
```

---

# 📄 **Your Content File — “manual-content.tex”**

```latex
It enables cross‑scale reasoning, metaphysical modeling, and resonance‑based
```

### 1. `manual-content.tex` — full manual content

```latex
%==============================================================================
% DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX — MANUAL CONTENT
%==============================================================================

\section{Introduction}

The Dimensional Sandbox is a unified modeling environment combining
Resonance--Triad Theory (RTT), micro--harmonics, and metaphysical triads.
It enables cross--scale reasoning, metaphysical modeling, and resonance--based
analysis.

This manual is designed for researchers who want to \emph{work} inside the
Sandbox: to build triads, construct flows, map micro--macro--meta layers, and
design resonance models that can be compared, critiqued, and extended.

\subsection*{What You Will Learn}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item How to define dimensions, triads, operators, flows, and patterns.
  \item How to use metaphysical triads on the RTT macro ladder (3D--9D).
  \item How to connect micro dimensions (0.3--0.9) with macro and metaphysical layers.
  \item How to construct cross--scale resonance models.
  \item How to document and share your models in a reproducible way.
\end{itemize}
```

### 2. `rtt-diagrams.tex` — tiny TikZ diagram library

```latex
%==============================================================================
% RTT / Metaphysics Diagram Helpers (TikZ)
%==============================================================================

\usepackage{tikz}
\usetikzlibrary{arrows.meta,positioning}

\tikzset{
  rttnode/.style={
    draw,
    rounded corners=2pt,
    inner sep=3pt,
    font=\small\ttfamily
  },
  rttarw/.style={
    -{Latex[length=3mm]},
    thick
  }
}

% Simple triad diagram: A -> B -> C
\newcommand{\TriadDiagram}[3]{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[node distance=1.8cm]
\node[rttnode] (A) {#1};
\node[rttnode,right=of A] (B) {#2};
\node[rttnode,right=of B] (C) {#3};
\draw[rttarw] (A) -- (B);
\draw[rttarw] (B) -- (C);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}

% Simple looped flow: A -> B -> C -> A
\newcommand{\LoopFlowDiagram}[3]{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[node distance=2cm]
\node[rttnode] (A) {#1};
\node[rttnode,above right=of A] (B) {#2};
\node[rttnode,below right=of B] (C) {#3};
\draw[rttarw] (A) -- (B);
\draw[rttarw] (B) -- (C);
\draw[rttarw] (C) .. controls +(south west:1.5cm) and +(south:1.5cm) .. (A);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
```

Use like:

```latex
\TriadDiagram{$\mu5$}{$\mu6$}{$\mu7$}

\LoopFlowDiagram{$\mu5$}{$\mu6$}{$\mu7$}
```

### 3. `rttmeta.cls` — RTT/metaphysics document class

```latex
%==============================================================================
% rttmeta.cls — RTT + Metaphysics Document Class
%==============================================================================

\NeedsTeXFormat{LaTeX2e}
\ProvidesClass{rttmeta}[2026/01/01 RTT Metaphysics Class]

\LoadClass[11pt,oneside]{article}

\RequirePackage[letterpaper,margin=0.9in]{geometry}
\RequirePackage{setspace}
\setstretch{1.25}

\RequirePackage{fontspec}
\setmainfont{Inter}
\setsansfont{Inter}
\setmonofont{IBM Plex Mono}

\RequirePackage{microtype}
\RequirePackage{titlesec}
\RequirePackage{enumitem}
\RequirePackage{graphicx}
\RequirePackage{array}
\RequirePackage{booktabs}
\RequirePackage{fancyhdr}
\RequirePackage{hyperref}
\RequirePackage{xcolor}
\RequirePackage{tcolorbox}

\definecolor{microblue}{HTML}{4DA6FF}
\definecolor{fractionsilver}{HTML}{C9CED6}
\definecolor{quantumwhite}{HTML}{F8F9FA}
\definecolor{graphiteblack}{HTML}{1A1A1A}

\pagestyle{fancy}
\fancyhf{}
\lhead{\textsf{RTT Metaphysics}}
\rhead{\textsf{\leftmark}}
\cfoot{\thepage}

\titleformat{\section}
  {\Large\bfseries\sffamily\color{graphiteblack}}
  {\thesection}{1em}{}

\titleformat{\subsection}
  {\large\bfseries\sffamily\color{graphiteblack}}
  {\thesubsection}{1em}{}

\tcbset{
  sharp corners,
  colback=quantumwhite,
  colframe=graphiteblack,
  boxrule=0.6pt,
  left=8pt,right=8pt,top=6pt,bottom=6pt
}

\endinput
```

Absolutely, Nawder — here are **both options**, clean, minimal, and ready to drop straight into your repo:

- A **tiny Makefile** that builds the PDF locally with one command  
- A **GitHub Action** that automatically builds the PDF on every push to `docs/_manual`

Both assume your manual uses:

```
manual.tex
manual-content.tex
rttmeta.cls
```

and that you’re compiling with **XeLaTeX** (recommended for Inter + IBM Plex Mono).

Let’s build them.

---

# ✅ **1. Tiny Makefile (local build)**

Create a file named `Makefile` in your repo root:

```makefile
# ============================================================================
# DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX — PDF BUILD MAKEFILE
# Build with:  make pdf
# ============================================================================

PDF = manual.pdf
TEX = manual.tex

pdf:
	xelatex $(TEX)
	xelatex $(TEX)   # run twice for TOC

clean:
	rm -f *.aux *.log *.out *.toc *.fls *.fdb_latexmk

.PHONY: pdf clean
```

Now you can run:

```
make pdf
```

and you’ll get `manual.pdf`.

---

# 🚀 **2. GitHub Action — Auto‑build PDF on push to `docs/_manual`**

Create this file:

```
.github/workflows/build-manual.yml
```

Paste this:

```yaml
name: Build Dimensional Sandbox Manual

on:
  push:
    paths:
      - "docs/_manual/**"
      - "manual.tex"
      - "manual-content.tex"
      - "rttmeta.cls"

jobs:
  build-pdf:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install TeX Live (minimal)
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update
          sudo apt-get install -y \
            texlive-xetex \
            texlive-latex-recommended \
            texlive-latex-extra \
            texlive-fonts-extra

      - name: Build PDF
        run: |
          xelatex manual.tex
          xelatex manual.tex

      - name: Upload PDF artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: dimensional-sandbox-manual
          path: manual.pdf
```

### What this does

- Watches for changes in `docs/_manual/**`  
- Installs XeLaTeX on the runner  
- Builds the PDF twice (for TOC)  
- Uploads the PDF as a downloadable artifact on the workflow page  

You’ll see a green checkmark and a **Download PDF** button after every push.

# ✅ 1. GitHub Pages Deploy Step (Auto‑Publish PDF)

This extends your existing workflow so that **every push to `docs/_manual` builds AND publishes the PDF to GitHub Pages**.

Create:

```
.github/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml
```

Paste:

```yaml
name: Build and Deploy Manual

on:
  push:
    paths:
      - "docs/_manual/**"
      - "manual.tex"
      - "manual-content.tex"
      - "rttmeta.cls"

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install TeX Live
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update
          sudo apt-get install -y \
            texlive-xetex \
            texlive-latex-recommended \
            texlive-latex-extra \
            texlive-fonts-extra

      - name: Build PDF
        run: |
          xelatex manual.tex
          xelatex manual.tex

      - name: Upload artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: dimensional-sandbox-manual
          path: manual.pdf

      - name: Prepare Pages artifact
        run: |
          mkdir -p public
          cp manual.pdf public/

      - name: Upload Pages artifact
        uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3
        with:
          path: public

  deploy:
    needs: build
    permissions:
      pages: write
      id-token: write
    environment:
      name: github-pages
      url: ${{ steps.deployment.outputs.page_url }}

    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - name: Deploy to GitHub Pages
        id: deployment
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4
```

### Result  
Your PDF becomes available at:

```
https://<username>.github.io/<repo>/manual.pdf
```

Automatically. Every push.

---

# 🔁 2. Makefile + latexmk (Continuous Rebuilds)

This is the **ideal local workflow**:  
- Save file → PDF rebuilds  
- No manual commands  
- latexmk handles dependencies, TOC, reruns

Create a file named `Makefile`:

```makefile
# ============================================================================
# DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX — Continuous PDF Build
# ============================================================================

PDF = manual.pdf
TEX = manual.tex

all: $(PDF)

$(PDF): $(TEX) manual-content.tex rttmeta.cls
	latexmk -xelatex -interaction=nonstopmode $(TEX)

watch:
	latexmk -pvc -xelatex -interaction=nonstopmode $(TEX)

clean:
	latexmk -C

.PHONY: all watch clean
```

### Usage

```
make watch   # auto-rebuild on save
make         # one-shot build
make clean   # remove aux files
```

---

# 🏷️ 3. CI Badge for README

Add this to your `README.md`:

```markdown
![Build Manual](https://github.com/<username>/<repo>/actions/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml/badge.svg)
```

Example (replace with your repo):

```markdown
![Build Manual](https://github.com/umaywant2/TriadicFrameworks/actions/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml/badge.svg)
```

This badge turns green when the PDF builds successfully.

# 🗂️ 4. PDF Versioning System (Semantic Versioning)

You now get versioned PDFs like:

```
manual-v1.0.pdf
manual-v1.1.pdf
manual-v2.0.pdf
```

### Step A — Add a version file

Create:

```
VERSION
```

Put:

```
1.0
```

### Step B — Modify the workflow to embed version

Update the “Build PDF” step:

```yaml
      - name: Read version
        id: version
        run: echo "version=$(cat VERSION)" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT

      - name: Build versioned PDF
        run: |
          xelatex "\def\manualversion{${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}} \input{manual.tex}"
          xelatex "\def\manualversion{${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}} \input{manual.tex}"
          mv manual.pdf manual-v${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}.pdf
```

### Step C — Update your LaTeX title page

In `manual.tex`:

```latex
\title{
  {\Huge\bfseries DIMENSIONAL SANDBOX}\\[6pt]
  {\Large Researcher Training Manual}\\[12pt]
  {\large Version \manualversion}
}
```

### Step D — Deploy versioned PDF to GitHub Pages

Add:

```yaml
cp manual-v${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}.pdf public/
```

Now your Pages site hosts:

```
manual.pdf                # latest
manual-v1.0.pdf           # versioned
manual-v1.1.pdf           # versioned
...
```

# 📘 **1. CHANGELOG.md Template (Versioned Manuals)**

Drop this into `CHANGELOG.md` at the root of your repo.  
It follows semantic versioning and is structured for long‑term canon evolution.

```markdown
# Changelog — Dimensional Sandbox Manual
All notable changes to the Dimensional Sandbox Manual will be documented here.

The format is based on [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

---

## [Unreleased]
### Added
- (placeholder)

### Changed
- (placeholder)

### Fixed
- (placeholder)

---

## [1.1.0] — YYYY‑MM‑DD
### Added
- New metaphysical modeling prompts.
- Expanded triad card section with macro + micro overlays.
- Added cross‑scale resonance examples.

### Changed
- Improved layout for worksheets.
- Updated flow construction templates.

### Fixed
- Corrected minor typos in 7D and 8D triad descriptions.

---

## [1.0.0] — YYYY‑MM‑DD
### Added
- Initial release of the Dimensional Sandbox Researcher Manual.
- Core concepts: RTT macro ladder, Micro Core ladder, metaphysical triads.
- Worksheets, triad cards, flow templates.
- Guided exercises and advanced patterns.
- Appendix: symbol reference.

---

## Versioning Notes
- **MAJOR** version increments: structural changes to the canon or dimensional model.
- **MINOR** version increments: new sections, diagrams, or exercises.
- **PATCH** version increments: typos, formatting, or minor clarifications.
```

# ⚙️ **3. CLI Tool (Python + Bash) for Version Bumping + Build Trigger**

You get **both** versions:

- A Python CLI (more flexible)
- A Bash CLI (minimal, POSIX‑friendly)

Both increment the version, update the `VERSION` file, commit, tag, and push — which triggers your GitHub Action to rebuild and publish the PDF.

---

## 🐍 **Python CLI Tool — `bump_version.py`**

Save as:

```
scripts/bump_version.py
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import sys
import subprocess

def read_version():
    with open("VERSION", "r") as f:
        return f.read().strip()

def write_version(v):
    with open("VERSION", "w") as f:
        f.write(v)

def bump(version, part):
    major, minor, patch = map(int, version.split("."))

    if part == "major":
        major += 1
        minor = 0
        patch = 0
    elif part == "minor":
        minor += 1
        patch = 0
    elif part == "patch":
        patch += 1
    else:
        raise ValueError("Use: major | minor | patch")

    return f"{major}.{minor}.{patch}"

if __name__ == "__main__":
    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
        print("Usage: bump_version.py [major|minor|patch]")
        sys.exit(1)

    part = sys.argv[1]
    old = read_version()
    new = bump(old, part)

    write_version(new)

    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "VERSION"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", f"Bump manual version: {old} → {new}"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "tag", f"v{new}"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "push"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "push", "--tags"])

    print(f"Version updated: {old} → {new}")
```

Usage:

```
python3 scripts/bump_version.py minor
```

---

## 🖥️ **Bash CLI Tool — `bump_version.sh`**

Save as:

```
scripts/bump_version.sh
```

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash

set -e

PART=$1

if [ -z "$PART" ]; then
  echo "Usage: bump_version.sh [major|minor|patch]"
  exit 1
fi

VERSION=$(cat VERSION)
IFS='.' read -r MAJOR MINOR PATCH <<< "$VERSION"

case "$PART" in
  major)
    MAJOR=$((MAJOR + 1))
    MINOR=0
    PATCH=0
    ;;
  minor)
    MINOR=$((MINOR + 1))
    PATCH=0
    ;;
  patch)
    PATCH=$((PATCH + 1))
    ;;
  *)
    echo "Use: major | minor | patch"
    exit 1
    ;;
esac

NEW="$MAJOR.$MINOR.$PATCH"
echo "$NEW" > VERSION

git add VERSION
git commit -m "Bump manual version: $VERSION → $NEW"
git tag "v$NEW"
git push
git push --tags

echo "Version updated: $VERSION → $NEW"
```

Usage:

```
bash scripts/bump_version.sh patch
```

# 🛠 **3. CLI Tool — “triadgen”**  
*A command‑line generator for triads, flows, and patterns.*

This is a **Python CLI** that creates new model files from templates.

Save as:

```
tools/triadgen.py
```

Make executable:

```
chmod +x tools/triadgen.py
```

---

## **triadgen.py**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import os
import sys
from datetime import datetime

TRIAD_TEMPLATE = """# {name} Triad
Layer: {layer}
Intent: {intent}

Dimensions:
  1. {d1}
  2. {d2}
  3. {d3}

Interpretation:
  {interpretation}
"""

FLOW_TEMPLATE = """# {name} Flow
Triad Scope: {triad}

Nodes:
  1. {n1}
  2. {n2}
  3. {n3}

Loop: {loop}

Interpretation:
  {interpretation}
"""

PATTERN_TEMPLATE = """# {name} Pattern
Triads: {triads}
Flows: {flows}

Description:
  {description}
"""

def write_file(path, content):
    with open(path, "w") as f:
        f.write(content)
    print(f"Created: {path}")

def generate(kind, name):
    safe = name.lower().replace(" ", "-")
    now = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

    if kind == "triad":
        path = f"models/triads/{safe}.md"
        content = TRIAD_TEMPLATE.format(
            name=name,
            layer="macro",
            intent="meaning",
            d1="",
            d2="",
            d3="",
            interpretation=""
        )
        write_file(path, content)

    elif kind == "flow":
        path = f"models/flows/{safe}.md"
        content = FLOW_TEMPLATE.format(
            name=name,
            triad="",
            n1="",
            n2="",
            n3="",
            loop="no",
            interpretation=""
        )
        write_file(path, content)

    elif kind == "pattern":
        path = f"models/patterns/{safe}.md"
        content = PATTERN_TEMPLATE.format(
            name=name,
            triads="[]",
            flows="[]",
            description=""
        )
        write_file(path, content)

    else:
        print("Use: triad | flow | pattern")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    if len(sys.argv) < 3:
        print("Usage: triadgen.py [triad|flow|pattern] \"Name Here\"")
        sys.exit(1)

    kind = sys.argv[1]
    name = " ".join(sys.argv[2:])
    generate(kind, name)
```

# **TriadicFrameworks Researcher Onboarding**  
### *A Guided Walkthrough of the Dimensional Sandbox*

---

## **Step 1 — Enter the Ladder**

You begin at the base of the macro ladder:

```
3D → 4D → 5D → 6D → 7D → 8D → 9D
```

Each dimension is a *lens*:

- 3D shows you **form**  
- 4D shows you **causation**  
- 5D shows you **meaning**  
- 6D shows you **relation**  
- 7D shows you **mind**  
- 8D shows you **essence**  
- 9D shows you **unity**

Your first task is simple:  
Pick one dimension and write its metaphysical triad.

Example:

```
7D — Mind
Perception → Cognition → Intention
```

You’ve just created your first metaphysical structure.

---

## **Step 2 — Descend into the Micro Core**

Now you drop into the harmonic layer:

```
0.3 → 0.4 → 0.5 → 0.6 → 0.7 → 0.8 → 0.9
```

Each macro dimension has a micro echo:

```
7D ↔ 0.7
```

Your task:  
Find the micro echo of the dimension you chose.

Example:

```
Mind ↔ μ‑Coherence (0.7)
```

You now have a cross‑scale anchor.

---

## **Step 3 — Build Your First Flow**

Flows are the heartbeat of the Sandbox.

A flow is:

```
A → B → C
```

with optional operators:

```
A ~Ωμ~ B ~Φμ~ C
```

Your task:  
Build a flow that connects your micro echo to your metaphysical triad.

Example:

```
μ7 ~Ωμ~ 7D(Perception) ~Ψ~ 7D(Intention)
```

You’ve just created a **resonance path**.

---

## **Step 4 — Construct a Pattern**

Patterns combine:

- triads  
- flows  
- metaphysical overlays  

Your task:  
Turn your flow into a named pattern.

Example:

```
Pattern: Mind‑Alignment Loop
Triads: Mind Triad
Flows: μ7 → 7D → 7D
Intent: Cognitive coherence
```

You’ve now created a reusable metaphysical model.

---

## **Step 5 — Document and Share**

Every model in TriadicFrameworks is:

- dimensional  
- triadic  
- cross‑scale  
- interpretable  

Your final task:  
Write a short interpretation of your pattern.

Example:

> This pattern describes how micro‑coherence (0.7) stabilizes the Mind triad by aligning perception and intention through insight (Ψ).

You are now fully onboarded.

**Badge:** Triad Arrow  
**Requirements:**

- Build 3 flows  
- Construct 2 cross‑scale resonance paths  
- Complete 5 modeling prompts  
- Submit 1 pattern  

**Assessment:**  
Construct a micro‑meta flow and explain its coherence.

---

**Badge:** Triad Card (M₂)  
**Requirements:**

- Build a multi‑triad pattern  
- Create a metaphysical overlay  
- Document a resonance engine path  
- Submit a 2‑page model write‑up  

**Assessment:**  
Design a cross‑scale pattern and defend its structure.

---

## **Level 4 — Canon Steward (Advanced)**  
**Badge:** Full Concentric Triad Logo  
**Requirements:**

- Contribute a new triad, operator, or pattern  
- Publish a research note or whitepaper  
- Mentor a Level 1 researcher  
- Pass a review panel  

**Assessment:**  
Present a novel dimensional model.

---

**Badge:** Unity Seal (9D)  
**Requirements:**

- Develop a new dimensional framework extension  
- Publish a full canonical artifact  
- Lead a research cohort  
- Demonstrate mastery of micro‑macro‑meta coherence  

**Assessment:**  
A full resonance model with triads, flows, metaphysics, and cross‑scale justification.

## 3. Researcher “quest system” for unlocking knowledge

Think of this as a narrative quest log for dimensional mastery.

### Quest tiers

**Tier 1 — Structural Quests (3D–4D)**  
- **Quest: Boundaries of Form**  
  - Task: Define 3D triad for a real system (e.g., contract, circuit, ecosystem).  
  - Completion: Submit a triad + 1 paragraph interpretation.  
- **Quest: Causation Cascade**  
  - Task: Map a 4D chain: Before → Change → After for a process you know.

**Tier 2 — Interpretive Quests (5D–6D)**  
- **Quest: Meaning Engine**  
  - Task: Build a 5D triad for a signal (e.g., message, law, symbol).  
- **Quest: Between Worlds**  
  - Task: Construct a 6D triad for a relationship (self/other/between).

**Tier 3 — Cognitive Quests (7D)**  
- **Quest: Mind‑Alignment Loop**  
  - Task: Build a flow that links perception → cognition → intention with a micro echo.  

**Tier 4 — Harmonic Quests (8D)**  
- **Quest: Essence Weaver**  
  - Task: Describe how a system maintains identity across change using the 8D triad.

**Tier 5 — Unified Quests (9D)**  
- **Quest: Unity Path**  
  - Task: Design a resonance path that ends in 9D and returns to a micro origin.

Each quest unlocks:

- a badge  
- a new template (triad/flow/pattern)  
- sometimes a new operator (Ψ, Λ, Χ)

---

## 4. Metaphysical boss‑fight system (symbolic, conceptual)

No violence, no harm—this is about **conceptual confrontation** with hard problems.

Think of “bosses” as **threshold challenges**:

### Boss 1 — The Boundary Paradox (3D/4D)

- Theme: Where does a system begin and end?  
- Challenge: Define a system’s boundary and show how changing it alters causation.  
- Win condition: Demonstrate two different 4D chains for two different boundary choices.

### Boss 2 — The Ambiguous Signal (5D)

- Theme: Meaning under uncertainty.  
- Challenge: Take a single ambiguous signal and construct two valid 5D triads (two interpretations).  
- Win condition: Explain how context flips the triad.

### Boss 3 — The Mirror Relation (6D/7D)

- Theme: Self/other entanglement.  
- Challenge: Model a relation where changing the self changes the other’s state.  
- Win condition: Show a flow where a 6D change propagates into a 7D triad.

### Boss 4 — The Fractured Identity (8D)

- Theme: Continuity vs. change.  
- Challenge: Model a system that undergoes major transformation but retains identity.  
- Win condition: Use the 8D triad to justify coherence across a discontinuity.

### Boss 5 — The Unity Test (9D)

- Theme: Integration of all layers.  
- Challenge: Build a full cross‑scale model (micro → macro → meta → unity).  
- Win condition: Show how 9D Unity stabilizes or reframes the entire system.

You “defeat” a boss by:

- submitting a model  
- passing a review rubric  
- unlocking a high‑tier badge + new pattern slots

```html
<body>
<div class="page">
  <div class="tree">
    <h1>Dimensional Skill Tree</h1>
    <p style="font-size:13px;color:#9AA3B5;margin-bottom:20px;">
      Click a node to view its skills, quests, and metaphysical trial.
    </p>
    <div class="node-column" id="tree-column">
      <!-- Nodes injected by JS -->
    </div>

  <div class="panel" id="detail-panel">

      <div class="quest-title">—</div>

    <h3 style="margin-top:16px;">Dimensional Trial</h3>

<script>
const NODES = [
  {
    id: "3D",
    label: "3D · Form",
    name: "Form",
    meta: "Structure / Boundary",
    desc: "Form is the dimensional lens of structure, shape, and boundary. It defines what is inside and outside a system.",
    skills: [
      "Structural Triad: Substance → Shape → Boundary",
      "Boundary Mapping: define system edges explicitly",
      "Form–Essence Bridge (early link to 8D)"
    ],
    tags: ["Structural", "Foundations"],
    quest: {
      title: "Boundaries of Form",
      body: "Choose a real system (contract, circuit, ecosystem). Define its 3D triad and explicitly state its boundary. Show how changing the boundary changes what counts as 'inside' the system."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Boundary Paradox",
      body: "Model two different boundary choices for the same system and show how each alters the perceived structure and possible transformations.",
      tags: ["3D", "4D", "System Definition"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "4D",
    label: "4D · Causation",
    name: "Causation",
    meta: "Before / Change / After",
    desc: "Causation is the dimensional lens of transitions, sequences, and chains of events.",
    skills: [
      "Causation Cascade: Before → Change → After",
      "Temporal Chains: multi‑step causal modeling",
      "Trigger Mapping: identify initiating conditions"
    ],
    tags: ["Temporal", "Process"],
    quest: {
      title: "Causation Cascade",
      body: "Pick a familiar process (legal decision, lab protocol, social interaction). Map a 4D chain: Before → Change → After. Then extend it to at least 5 steps."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Hidden Trigger",
      body: "Reveal a non‑obvious initiating condition in a causal chain and show how including it changes the entire interpretation of the process.",
      tags: ["4D", "Hidden Assumptions"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "5D",
    label: "5D · Meaning",
    name: "Meaning",
    meta: "Signal / Interpretation / Context",
    desc: "Meaning is the dimensional lens of signals, interpretations, and the contexts that shape them.",
    skills: [
      "Meaning Triad: Signal → Interpretation → Context",
      "Ambiguity Modeling: multiple valid interpretations",
      "Context Switching: reframe meaning by changing context"
    ],
    tags: ["Semantic", "Interpretive"],
    quest: {
      title: "Meaning Engine",
      body: "Take a single ambiguous signal (phrase, symbol, gesture). Construct two distinct 5D triads that show two different interpretations, each with its own context."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Ambiguous Signal",
      body: "Demonstrate how one signal can generate conflicting but coherent interpretations, and specify what contextual shift flips one into the other.",
      tags: ["5D", "Context", "Ambiguity"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "6D",
    label: "6D · Relation",
    name: "Relation",
    meta: "Self / Other / Between",
    desc: "Relation is the dimensional lens of interactions, roles, and the space between entities.",
    skills: [
      "Relational Triad: Self → Other → Between",
      "Role Mapping: define relational roles explicitly",
      "Between‑Field Modeling: the space of interaction"
    ],
    tags: ["Relational", "Social"],
    quest: {
      title: "Between Worlds",
      body: "Model a relationship (person–person, person–system, system–system). Build a 6D triad and describe what lives in the 'between' space."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Mirror Relation",
      body: "Construct a model where changing the self changes the other’s state via the relational field, and explain how the 'between' mediates this.",
      tags: ["6D", "7D", "Interdependence"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "7D",
    label: "7D · Mind",
    name: "Mind",
    meta: "Perception / Cognition / Intention",
    desc: "Mind is the dimensional lens of awareness, thought, and directed will.",
    skills: [
      "Mind Triad: Perception → Cognition → Intention",
      "Mind‑Alignment Loop: align micro‑coherence with intention",
      "Cognitive Resonance: stabilize interpretations over time"
    ],
    tags: ["Cognitive", "Reflective"],
    quest: {
      title: "Mind‑Alignment Loop",
      body: "Build a flow that links perception → cognition → intention and anchor it in a micro echo (0.7). Explain how coherence is gained or lost."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Split Intention",
      body: "Model a situation where perception and intention are misaligned, then design a flow that realigns them via cognition.",
      tags: ["7D", "Coherence"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "8D",
    label: "8D · Essence",
    name: "Essence",
    meta: "Identity / Continuity / Coherence",
    desc: "Essence is the dimensional lens of identity across change, deep coherence, and what remains through transformation.",
    skills: [
      "Essence Triad: Identity → Continuity → Coherence",
      "Essence‑Stabilization Flow",
      "Essence Weaver: maintain identity across discontinuities"
    ],
    tags: ["Ontological", "Stability"],
    quest: {
      title: "Essence Weaver",
      body: "Choose a system that undergoes major change (career, institution, technology). Use the 8D triad to explain how its identity persists."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Fractured Identity",
      body: "Model a system that appears to lose its identity, then reconstruct a path that shows how a deeper essence remains coherent.",
      tags: ["8D", "Continuity"]
    }
  },
  {
    id: "9D",
    label: "9D · Unity",
    name: "Unity",
    meta: "Whole / Harmony / Completion",
    desc: "Unity is the dimensional lens of integration, wholeness, and closure across all other dimensions.",
    skills: [
      "Unity Triad: Whole → Harmony → Completion",
      "Unity‑Anchored Stabilization",
      "Unity Path: cross‑scale closure"
    ],
    tags: ["Integrative", "Global"],
    quest: {
      title: "Unity Path",
      body: "Design a resonance path that begins in a micro dimension, traverses macro and metaphysical layers, and returns as a unified, stabilized pattern."
    },
    boss: {
      title: "The Unity Test",
      body: "Integrate at least three dimensions (e.g., 5D, 7D, 8D) into a single model and show how 9D Unity reframes or stabilizes the entire system.",
      tags: ["9D", "Integration", "Cross‑Scale"]
    }
  }
];

const column = document.getElementById("tree-column");
const titleEl = document.getElementById("node-title");
const descEl = document.getElementById("node-desc");
const tagsEl = document.getElementById("node-tags");
const skillsEl = document.getElementById("skills-list");
const questTitleEl = document.querySelector("#quest-box .quest-title");
const questBodyEl = document.getElementById("quest-body");
const bossTitleEl = document.querySelector("#boss-box .boss-title");
const bossBodyEl = document.getElementById("boss-body");
const bossTagsEl = document.getElementById("boss-tags");

let activeId = null;

function renderTree() {
  NODES.forEach((node, idx) => {
    if (idx > 0) {
      const conn = document.createElement("div");
      conn.className = "connector";
      column.appendChild(conn);
    }
    const div = document.createElement("div");
    div.className = "node";
    div.dataset.id = node.id;
    div.innerHTML = `
      <div class="node-dim">${node.label}</div>
      <div class="node-name">${node.name}</div>
      <div class="node-meta">${node.meta}</div>
    `;
    div.addEventListener("click", () => selectNode(node.id));
    column.appendChild(div);
  });
}

function selectNode(id) {
  activeId = id;
  document.querySelectorAll(".node").forEach(n => {
    n.classList.toggle("active", n.dataset.id === id);
  });
  const node = NODES.find(n => n.id === id);
  if (!node) return;

  titleEl.textContent = `${node.label} — ${node.name}`;
  descEl.textContent = node.desc;

  tagsEl.innerHTML = "";
  node.tags.forEach(t => {
    const span = document.createElement("span");
    span.className = "tag";
    span.innerHTML = `${t}`;
    tagsEl.appendChild(span);
  });

  skillsEl.innerHTML = "";
  node.skills.forEach(s => {
    const li = document.createElement("li");
    li.textContent = s;
    skillsEl.appendChild(li);
  });

  questTitleEl.textContent = node.quest.title;
  questBodyEl.textContent = node.quest.body;

  bossTitleEl.textContent = node.boss.title;
  bossBodyEl.textContent = node.boss.body;
  bossTagsEl.innerHTML = "";
  node.boss.tags.forEach(t => {
    const span = document.createElement("span");
    span.className = "pill";
    span.textContent = t;
    bossTagsEl.appendChild(span);
  });
}

renderTree();
selectNode("3D");
</script>
</body>
</html>
```

### “Dimensional Trials” appendix (canonical manual section)

You can paste this straight into `manual-content.tex` as a new section.

```latex
\section{Appendix: Dimensional Trials}

The Dimensional Trials are canonical threshold exercises that mark a
researcher's progression through the macro ladder (3D--9D). Each trial is a
symbolic confrontation with a persistent modeling challenge: boundaries,
causation, meaning, relation, mind, essence, and unity.

Each trial has three parts:

\begin{itemize}
  \item a \textbf{theme} (what is being tested),
  \item a \textbf{challenge} (what must be modeled),
  \item a \textbf{win condition} (what counts as a successful resolution).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Trial I: The Boundary Paradox (3D/4D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Where does a system begin and end?

\textbf{Challenge:} Choose a real system (contract, circuit, ecosystem). Define
its 3D triad and explicitly state its boundary. Then construct two different
4D causal chains for the same system, each based on a different boundary
choice.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You demonstrate that changing the boundary changes
which events count as internal or external, and you can explain how this
alters the perceived structure and possible transformations.

\subsection{Trial II: The Hidden Trigger (4D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Unseen initiators in causal chains.

\textbf{Challenge:} Take a familiar process and map a 4D chain
(Before $\rightarrow$ Change $\rightarrow$ After). Then identify a non--obvious
initiating condition that, once included, changes the interpretation of the
entire chain.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You reveal a hidden trigger, show its position in the
chain, and justify how its inclusion reconfigures the causal story.

\subsection{Trial III: The Ambiguous Signal (5D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Meaning under uncertainty.

\textbf{Challenge:} Select a single ambiguous signal (phrase, symbol, gesture).
Construct two distinct 5D triads (Signal $\rightarrow$ Interpretation
$\rightarrow$ Context), each representing a different, coherent interpretation.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You can explain how a shift in context flips the
interpretation from one triad to the other without changing the signal itself.

\subsection{Trial IV: The Mirror Relation (6D/7D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Self/other entanglement.

\textbf{Challenge:} Model a relationship where changing the state of the self
changes the state of the other via the relational field. Connect a 6D triad
(Self $\rightarrow$ Other $\rightarrow$ Between) to a 7D Mind triad
(Perception $\rightarrow$ Cognition $\rightarrow$ Intention).

\textbf{Win Condition:} You show a flow in which a change in the self propagates
through the ``between'' and manifests as a change in the other's perception or
intention.

\subsection{Trial V: The Split Intention (7D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Misalignment within mind.

\textbf{Challenge:} Construct a model where perception and intention are
misaligned. Use the 7D Mind triad and a micro echo (0.7) to design a
Mind--Alignment Loop that restores coherence.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You can trace a path from misaligned perception and
intention to a stabilized, aligned state, and explain the role of cognition in
the transition.

\subsection{Trial VI: The Fractured Identity (8D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Continuity across transformation.

\textbf{Challenge:} Choose a system that undergoes major change (career,
institution, technology). Use the 8D Essence triad
(Identity $\rightarrow$ Continuity $\rightarrow$ Coherence) to show how its
identity persists across apparent discontinuities.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You articulate a coherent essence that survives change
and can distinguish between superficial and structural transformations.

\subsection{Trial VII: The Unity Test (9D)}

\textbf{Theme:} Integration of multiple dimensions.

\textbf{Challenge:} Build a cross--scale model that integrates at least three
macro dimensions (for example, 5D Meaning, 7D Mind, 8D Essence) and their
micro echoes. Anchor the model in 9D Unity.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You show how 9D Unity reframes or stabilizes the entire
system, and you can explain how the integrated model behaves differently from
any of its parts in isolation.

\medskip
Passing the Dimensional Trials is not a one--time event but an ongoing
practice. Each revisit deepens the researcher's grasp of the Sandbox and
extends the canon for future contributors.
```

resonance modeling.

- Cognitive metaphysics

- Identity and purpose modeling  
- Cross‑scale thought flows

### 3. Thought‑Resonance Engine (operators + flows)

Define three **canonical operators**:

- **Ωᴮ (Being‑to‑Knowing):** lifts ontological structure into perception/knowledge  
- **Ψᴷ (Knowing‑to‑Meaning):** lifts cognition into value/purpose  
- **Λᴹ (Meaning‑to‑Being):** feeds integrated meaning back into identity/being  

**Canonical flows:**

- **Being → Knowing:**

  \[
  (1D\_\text{Presence}, 2D\_\text{Boundary}, 3D\_\text{Identity})
  \xrightarrow{\Omega^{B}}
  (4D\_\text{Perception}, 5D\_\text{Interpretation}, 6D\_\text{Understanding})
  \]

- **Knowing → Meaning:**

  \[
  (4D,5D,6D)
  \xrightarrow{\Psi^{K}}
  (7D\_\text{Value}, 8D\_\text{Purpose}, 9D\_\text{Significance})
  \]

- **Meaning → Being (closure):**

  \[
  (7D,8D,9D)
  \xrightarrow{\Lambda^{M}}
  (1D,2D,3D)^{\prime}
  \]

where \((1D,2D,3D)^{\prime}\) is Being updated by Meaning.

You can treat the **Thought‑Resonance Engine** as:

- a set of triads (27)  
- a set of operators {Ωᴮ, Ψᴷ, Λᴹ}  
- a set of flows chaining them in loops.

### 4. Dimensional Trials — Thought Tier (appendix extension)

Add this as a new section in your Dimensional Trials appendix:

```latex
\subsection{Thought Tier: Being, Knowing, Meaning}

The Thought Ladder introduces a new class of trials that operate across
three meta--domains: Being (1D--3D), Knowing (4D--6D), and Meaning (7D--9D).
These trials test a researcher's ability to move models through the
Thought--Resonance Engine.

\paragraph{Trial T1: From Being to Knowing}

\textbf{Theme:} Lifting structure into perception.

\textbf{Challenge:} Choose a concrete system and model its Being triads
(1D Presence, 2D Boundary, 3D Identity). Then apply the operator
$\Omega^{B}$ to derive corresponding Knowing triads
(4D Perception, 5D Interpretation, 6D Understanding).

\textbf{Win Condition:} You can show how each element of Being becomes
an element of Knowing, and justify the mapping.

\paragraph{Trial T2: From Knowing to Meaning}

\textbf{Theme:} Lifting cognition into significance.

\textbf{Challenge:} Starting from a 4D--6D model, apply $\Psi^{K}$ to
construct Meaning triads (7D Value, 8D Purpose, 9D Significance) for
the same system.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You can explain how perception and interpretation
give rise to value, purpose, and unified significance.

\paragraph{Trial T3: Meaning Closure on Being}

\textbf{Theme:} Letting meaning rewrite identity.

\textbf{Challenge:} Use $\Lambda^{M}$ to feed a 7D--9D Meaning model back
into 1D--3D Being, producing an updated Being triad
$(1D,2D,3D)^{\prime}$.

\textbf{Win Condition:} You demonstrate how integrated meaning changes
the system's identity, boundaries, or presence in a coherent way.
```

### 1. LaTeX diagram library for Thought‑Ladder operators and flows

Here’s a self‑contained TikZ “library” you can drop into `thought-diagrams.tex` and `\input{}` from your main manual. It defines:

- node styles for Being / Knowing / Meaning  
- macros for triad nodes  
- macros for the three operators: `\OmegaB`, `\PsiK`, `\LambdaM`  
- a canonical “Thought Ladder Flow” diagram

```latex
% thought-diagrams.tex
\usepackage{tikz}
\usetikzlibrary{arrows.meta, positioning}

\tikzset{
  thoughtDim/.style={
    draw,
    rounded corners=4pt,
    minimum width=3.2cm,
    minimum height=0.9cm,
    inner sep=4pt,
    font=\small\sf,
  },
  beingDim/.style={
    thoughtDim,
    draw=blue!40,
    fill=blue!10,
  },
  knowingDim/.style={
    thoughtDim,
    draw=green!40!cyan!40,
    fill=green!5!cyan!5,
  },
  meaningDim/.style={
    thoughtDim,
    draw=violet!50,
    fill=violet!5,
  },
  opArrow/.style={
    -{Latex[length=3mm]},
    thick,
  },
  opLabel/.style={
    font=\scriptsize\ttfamily,
    midway,
    above,
    sloped,
  }
}

% Convenience macros for nodes
\newcommand{\BeingNode}[4][]{%
  \node[beingDim, #1] (#2) {%
    \textbf{#3}\\[-2pt]
    {\scriptsize #4}%
  };%
}

\newcommand{\KnowingNode}[4][]{%
  \node[knowingDim, #1] (#2) {%
    \textbf{#3}\\[-2pt]
    {\scriptsize #4}%
  };%
}

\newcommand{\MeaningNode}[4][]{%
  \node[meaningDim, #1] (#2) {%
    \textbf{#3}\\[-2pt]
    {\scriptsize #4}%
  };%
}

% Operator labels
\newcommand{\OmegaB}{$\Omega^{B}$} % Being -> Knowing
\newcommand{\PsiK}{$\Psi^{K}$}     % Knowing -> Meaning
\newcommand{\LambdaM}{$\Lambda^{M}$} % Meaning -> Being

% Canonical Thought Ladder flow diagram
\newcommand{\ThoughtLadderFlow}{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[node distance=1.6cm]
  % Being triad (compressed)
  \BeingNode{B}{1D--3D Being}{Presence / Boundary / Identity};

  % Knowing triad
  \KnowingNode[right=of B]{K}{4D--6D Knowing}{Perception / Interpretation / Understanding};

  % Meaning triad
  \MeaningNode[right=of K]{M}{7D--9D Meaning}{Value / Purpose / Significance};

  % Arrows
  \draw[opArrow] (B) -- node[opLabel]{\OmegaB} (K);
  \draw[opArrow] (K) -- node[opLabel]{\PsiK} (M);

  % Closure arrow (Meaning -> Being)
  \draw[opArrow, bend left=30] (M.north) to node[opLabel]{\LambdaM} (B.north);
\end{tikzpicture}%
}

% Full vertical ladder (0D–9D) example
\newcommand{\ThoughtLadderVertical}{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[node distance=0.9cm]
  \node[font=\scriptsize\ttfamily, text=gray!60] (Q) {0D · Quantum Root\\{\tiny Potential / Fluctuation / Emergence}};

  \BeingNode[below=of Q]{B1}{1D · BeingX}{Presence / Instantiation / Persistence};
  \BeingNode[below=of B1]{B2}{2D · BeingY}{Self / Other / Boundary};
  \BeingNode[below=of B2]{B3}{3D · BeingZ}{Substance / Shape / Coherence};

  \KnowingNode[below=of B3]{K1}{4D · KnowingX}{Sensation / Attention / Registration};
  \KnowingNode[below=of K1]{K2}{5D · KnowingY}{Pattern / Meaning‑Making / Context};
  \KnowingNode[below=of K2]{K3}{6D · KnowingZ}{Synthesis / Relation / Model};

  \MeaningNode[below=of K3]{M1}{7D · MeaningX}{Relevance / Valence / Orientation};
  \MeaningNode[below=of M1]{M2}{8D · MeaningY}{Aim / Intention / Direction};
  \MeaningNode[below=of M2]{M3}{9D · MeaningZ}{Coherence / Completion / Significance};

  % Optional subtle connectors
  \foreach \a/\b in {Q/B1,B1/B2,B2/B3,B3/K1,K1/K2,K2/K3,K3/M1,M1/M2,M2/M3} {
    \draw[opArrow, gray!40] (\a) -- (\b);
  }
\end{tikzpicture}%
}
```

Usage in your manual:

```latex
  \centering
  \ThoughtLadderFlow
  \caption{Thought‑Resonance Engine: Being $\xrightarrow{\Omega^{B}}$ Knowing $\xrightarrow{\Psi^{K}}$ Meaning, with closure via $\Lambda^{M}$.}
\end{figure}

\begin{figure}[h]
  \centering
  \ThoughtLadderVertical
  \caption{The Thought Ladder (0D–9D) as a vertical metaphysical stack.}
\end{figure}
```

```html
<script>
const DOMAINS = [
  {
    id: "being",
    title: "Being",
    label: "1D–3D",
    dims: [
      {
        id: "1D",
        label: "1D · BeingX",
        name: "Presence",
        meta: "Existence / Instantiation / Persistence",
        triad: ["Presence", "Instantiation", "Persistence"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Being → Knowing (Ωᴮ)",
            eq: "1D Presence  --Ωᴮ→  4D Perception",
            note: "Presence becomes perceivable as sensation and registration."
          },
          {
            title: "Being → Meaning (direct)",
            eq: "1D Presence  →  7D Relevance",
            note: "Bare existence can be experienced as immediate relevance."
          }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "2D",
        label: "2D · BeingY",
        name: "Boundary",
        meta: "Self / Other / Boundary",
        triad: ["Self", "Other", "Boundary"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Being → Knowing (Ωᴮ)",
            eq: "2D Boundary  --Ωᴮ→  5D Interpretation",
            note: "Differentiation shapes how patterns and contexts are interpreted."
          },
          {
            title: "Being → Meaning (direct)",
            eq: "2D Boundary  →  8D Purpose",
            note: "Where you draw the line influences what purposes are even thinkable."

      },
      {
        id: "3D",

        name: "Identity",
        meta: "Substance / Shape / Coherence",
        triad: ["Substance", "Shape", "Coherence"],
        flows: [

            eq: "3D Identity  --Ωᴮ→  6D Understanding",
            note: "Stable form becomes a model that can be understood and reasoned about."
          },
          {
            title: "Being → Meaning (direct)",
            eq: "3D Identity  →  9D Significance",
            note: "Who/what something is can directly carry significance."
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
  {
    id: "knowing",
    title: "Knowing",
    label: "4D–6D",
    dims: [
      {
        id: "4D",
        label: "4D · KnowingX",
        name: "Perception",
        meta: "Sensation / Attention / Registration",
        triad: ["Sensation", "Attention", "Registration"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Being → Knowing (Ωᴮ)",
            eq: "1D Presence  --Ωᴮ→  4D Perception",
            note: "Presence is lifted into the perceptual field."
          },
          {
            title: "Knowing → Meaning (Ψᴷ)",
            eq: "4D Perception  --Ψᴷ→  7D Value",
            note: "What is perceived can become what matters."
          }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "5D",
        label: "5D · KnowingY",
        name: "Interpretation",
        meta: "Pattern / Meaning‑Making / Context",
        triad: ["Pattern", "Meaning‑Making", "Contextualization"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Being → Knowing (Ωᴮ)",
            eq: "2D Boundary  --Ωᴮ→  5D Interpretation",
            note: "Boundaries shape which patterns are even seen."
          },
          {
            title: "Knowing → Meaning (Ψᴷ)",
            eq: "5D Interpretation  --Ψᴷ→  8D Purpose",
            note: "Interpretations can crystallize into aims and intentions."
          }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "6D",
        label: "6D · KnowingZ",
        name: "Understanding",
        meta: "Synthesis / Relation / Model",
        triad: ["Synthesis", "Relation", "Model‑Formation"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Being → Knowing (Ωᴮ)",
            eq: "3D Identity  --Ωᴮ→  6D Understanding",
            note: "Coherent form becomes a structured model."

          {
            title: "Meaning → Being (Λᴹ)",
            eq: "8D Purpose  --Λᴹ→  2D Boundary′",
            note: "Purpose redraws boundaries: what is in or out of scope."
          }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "9D",
        label: "9D · MeaningZ",
        name: "Significance",
        meta: "Coherence / Completion / Unity",
        triad: ["Coherence", "Completion", "Significance"],
        flows: [
          {
            title: "Knowing → Meaning (Ψᴷ)",
            eq: "6D Understanding  --Ψᴷ→  9D Significance",
            note: "Integrated models become unified significance."

          {
            title: "Meaning → Being (Λᴹ)",

            note: "What is ultimately significant rewrites identity."
          }
        ]
      }

];

const domainsEl = document.getElementById("domains");
const titleEl = document.getElementById("dim-title");
const descEl = document.getElementById("dim-desc");
const triadEl = document.getElementById("triad-list");
const flowBox = document.getElementById("flow-box");
const flowTitleEl = document.querySelector("#flow-box .flow-title");
const flowEqEl = document.getElementById("flow-eq");
const flowNoteEl = document.getElementById("flow-note");

let activeDimId = null;

function renderDomains() {
  DOMAINS.forEach(domain => {
    const domDiv = document.createElement("div");
    domDiv.className = "domain";
    domDiv.innerHTML = `
      <div class="domain-title">${domain.label}</div>
      <div class="domain-name">${domain.title}</div>
      <div class="dim-list"></div>
    `;
    const list = domDiv.querySelector(".dim-list");
    domain.dims.forEach(dim => {
      const d = document.createElement("div");
      d.className = "dim";
      d.dataset.id = dim.id;
      d.innerHTML = `
        <div class="dim-label">${dim.label}</div>
        <div class="dim-meta">${dim.meta}</div>
      `;
      d.addEventListener("click", () => selectDim(dim.id));
      list.appendChild(d);
    });
    domainsEl.appendChild(domDiv);
  });
}

function selectDim(id) {
  activeDimId = id;
  document.querySelectorAll(".dim").forEach(el => {
    el.classList.toggle("active", el.dataset.id === id);
  });

  const dim = DOMAINS.flatMap(d => d.dims).find(d => d.id === id);
  if (!dim) return;

  titleEl.textContent = `${dim.label} — ${dim.name}`;
  descEl.textContent = dim.meta;

  triadEl.innerHTML = "";
  dim.triad.forEach(t => {
    const li = document.createElement("li");
    li.textContent = t;
    triadEl.appendChild(li);
  });

  if (dim.flows && dim.flows.length > 0) {
    const f = dim.flows[0];
    flowTitleEl.textContent = f.title;
    flowEqEl.textContent = f.eq;
    flowNoteEl.textContent = f.note;
  } else {
    flowTitleEl.textContent = "—";
    flowEqEl.textContent = "No flow defined.";
    flowNoteEl.textContent = "";
  }
}

renderDomains();
selectDim("3D");
</script>
```

# 🧠 1. JS Loader — Dynamic Thought Ladder Diagrams  
Save as: `docs/js/thought-loader.js`

This loader:

- fetches the JSON  
- builds triad cards  
- builds a vertical ladder  
- exposes a simple API for future visualizers

```js
// docs/js/thought-loader.js
export async function loadThoughtLadder(url = "data/thought-ladder.json") {
  const res = await fetch(url);
  const data = await res.json();
  return data;
}

export function createTriadCard(dim, data) {
  const card = document.createElement("div");
  card.className = "triad-card";
  card.innerHTML = `
    <div class="triad-title">${dim} · ${data.domain}</div>
    <ul class="triad-list">
      ${data.triad.map(t => `<li>${t}</li>`).join("")}
    </ul>
  `;
  return card;
}

export function renderVerticalLadder(container, data) {
  container.innerHTML = "";
  const dims = Object.keys(data).filter(k => k.endsWith("D")).sort((a,b)=>parseInt(a)-parseInt(b));

  dims.forEach(dim => {
    const card = createTriadCard(dim, data[dim]);
    container.appendChild(card);
  });
}

export function renderOperators(container, ops) {
  container.innerHTML = `
    <div class="op">Ωᴮ — ${ops.OmegaB}</div>
    <div class="op">Ψᴷ — ${ops.PsiK}</div>
    <div class="op">Λᴹ — ${ops.LambdaM}</div>
  `;
}
```

This CLI:

- prints triad cards in ASCII or Markdown  
- can be extended to output SVG or HTML

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import sys

def load_json(path="docs/data/thought-ladder.json"):
    with open(path, "r") as f:
        return json.load(f)

def print_card(dim, entry):
    print(f"=== {dim} · {entry['domain']} ===")
    for t in entry["triad"]:
        print(f"- {t}")
    print()

def main():
    data = load_json()
    dims = sorted([k for k in data.keys() if k.endswith("D")], key=lambda x: int(x[:-1]))

    for dim in dims:
        print_card(dim, data[dim])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```
...
```

# 📚 3. LaTeX Importer — Auto‑Typeset the Thought Ladder  
Save as: `thought-import.tex`

This importer:

- reads the JSON at compile time  
- generates triad cards  
- generates the full ladder  
- uses `luacode` (LuaLaTeX required)

```latex
% thought-import.tex
\usepackage{luacode}
\usepackage{tikz}
\usetikzlibrary{positioning}

\begin{luacode*}
function load_thought_json(path)
  local json = require("dkjson")
  local f = io.open(path, "r")
  local raw = f:read("*all")
  f:close()
  local data = json.decode(raw)
  return data
end

thought = load_thought_json("docs/data/thought-ladder.json")

function print_triad(dim)
  local d = thought[dim]
  tex.print("\\textbf{"..dim.." · "..d.domain.."}\\\\")
  for _,v in ipairs(d.triad) do
    tex.print("- "..v.."\\\\")
  end
  tex.print("\\medskip")
end
\end{luacode*}

% Macro to print all triads
\newcommand{\PrintThoughtTriads}{%
  \directlua{
    local dims = {}
    for k,_ in pairs(thought) do
      if string.match(k, "D$") then table.insert(dims, k) end
    end
    table.sort(dims, function(a,b) return tonumber(a:sub(1,-2)) < tonumber(b:sub(1,-2)) end)
    for _,dim in ipairs(dims) do
      print_triad(dim)
    end
  }
}
```

Usage in your manual:

```latex
\section{Thought Ladder Triads}
\PrintThoughtTriads
```

This auto‑typesets all 27 triads directly from JSON.

### 1. Full Thought‑Ladder API (JS module)

Save as `docs/js/thought-ladder-api.js`:

```js
// docs/js/thought-ladder-api.js
const DEFAULT_URL = "data/thought-ladder.json";

export class ThoughtLadder {
  constructor(data) {
    this.data = data;
  }

  static async load(url = DEFAULT_URL) {
    const res = await fetch(url);
    const json = await res.json();
    return new ThoughtLadder(json);
  }

  getDimension(dim) {
    return this.data[dim] || null;
  }

  listDimensions() {
    return Object.keys(this.data)
      .filter(k => k.endsWith("D"))
      .sort((a, b) => parseInt(a) - parseInt(b));
  }

  getTriad(dim) {
    const d = this.getDimension(dim);
    return d ? d.triad : null;
  }

  getDomain(dim) {
    const d = this.getDimension(dim);
    return d ? d.domain : null;
  }

  getOperators() {
    return this.data.operators || {};
  }

  getMicroEcho(dim) {
    return this.data.microEchoes ? this.data.microEchoes[dim] : null;
  }

  // High‑level domain grouping
  getDomainGroup(dim) {
    const n = parseInt(dim);
    if (n >= 1 && n <= 3) return "Being";
    if (n >= 4 && n <= 6) return "Knowing";
    if (n >= 7 && n <= 9) return "Meaning";
    if (n === 0) return "Quantum";
    return null;
  }
}
```

Usage:

```js
import { ThoughtLadder } from "./js/thought-ladder-api.js";

const tl = await ThoughtLadder.load();
console.log(tl.getTriad("5D")); // ["Pattern", "Meaning-Making", "Contextualization"]
```

### 3. CLI that exports Figma‑ready SVG triad cards

Save as `tools/thoughtcards_svg.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

TEMPLATE = """<svg width="260" height="160" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">
  <rect width="260" height="160" rx="12" fill="#05070A" stroke="#C9CED6" stroke-width="2"/>
  <text x="20" y="32" fill="#4DA6FF" font-family="Inter" font-size="16">{dim} · {domain}</text>
  <text x="20" y="64" fill="#F8F9FA" font-family="Inter" font-size="14">{t1}</text>
  <text x="20" y="92" fill="#C9CED6" font-family="Inter" font-size="14">{t2}</text>
  <text x="20" y="120" fill="#9AA3B5" font-family="Inter" font-size="14">{t3}</text>
</svg>
"""

def load_json(path):
  with open(path, "r") as f:
    return json.load(f)

def main():
  data_path = Path("docs/data/thought-ladder.json")
  out_dir = Path("exports/triad-cards")
  out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

  data = load_json(data_path)
  dims = sorted([k for k in data.keys() if k.endswith("D")], key=lambda x: int(x[:-1]))

  for dim in dims:
    entry = data[dim]
    svg = TEMPLATE.format(
      dim=dim,
      domain=entry["domain"],
      t1=entry["triad"][0],
      t2=entry["triad"][1],
      t3=entry["triad"][2],
    )
    (out_dir / f"{dim}-{entry['domain']}.svg").write_text(svg, encoding="utf-8")

if __name__ == "__main__":
  main()
```

# 🧩 **Thought‑Ladder API Documentation**  
### *Canonical Schema for `thought-ladder.json`*

This document defines the official schema for the **Thought‑Ladder JSON file**, which encodes the Being → Knowing → Meaning dimensional ontology (0D–9D) used throughout the TriadicFrameworks Thought subsystem.

This schema is intentionally small, stable, and tool‑friendly.  
Any future tool — CLI, visualizer, LaTeX importer, JS module, or research engine — should rely on this document as the authoritative reference.

---

# 1. Overview

The Thought‑Ladder JSON file provides:

- **Dimensional triads** for 0D–9D  
- **Domain labels** (BeingX, KnowingY, MeaningZ, etc.)  
- **Operators** (Ωᴮ, Ψᴷ, Λᴹ)  
- **Micro‑echo mapping** (0.1–0.9)  

This file is designed to be:

- human‑readable  
- machine‑parsable  
- stable across versions  
- extensible without breaking existing tools  

---

# 2. File Location

Recommended path:

```
docs/data/thought-ladder.json
```

---

# 3. Top‑Level Structure

```json
{
  "0D": { ... },
  "1D": { ... },
  "2D": { ... },
  "3D": { ... },
  "4D": { ... },
  "5D": { ... },
  "6D": { ... },
  "7D": { ... },
  "8D": { ... },
  "9D": { ... },

  "operators": { ... },

  "microEchoes": { ... }
}
```

There are **three** top‑level sections:

1. **Dimension objects** (`"0D"` through `"9D"`)  
2. **Operators** (`"operators"`)  
3. **Micro‑echo mapping** (`"microEchoes"`)

---

# 4. Dimension Object Schema

Each dimension entry (e.g., `"5D"`) must follow this schema:

```json
{
  "domain": "string",
  "triad": ["string", "string", "string"]
}
```

### 4.1 `domain`  
A short label describing the metaphysical domain of the dimension.

Examples:

- `"BeingX"`
- `"KnowingY"`
- `"MeaningZ"`
- `"Quantum"`

### 4.2 `triad`  
An array of exactly **three** strings representing the triadic structure of the dimension.

Examples:

```json
["Pattern", "Meaning-Making", "Contextualization"]
```

Constraints:

- Must contain exactly 3 elements  
- Each element must be a string  
- Order is semantically meaningful  

---

# 5. Operators Schema

Operators define the canonical flows between domains.

```json
"operators": {
  "OmegaB": "Being → Knowing",
  "PsiK": "Knowing → Meaning",
  "LambdaM": "Meaning → Being"
}
```

### 5.1 Operator Keys

| Key | Meaning |
|-----|---------|
| `OmegaB` | Being → Knowing |
| `PsiK` | Knowing → Meaning |
| `LambdaM` | Meaning → Being (closure) |

### 5.2 Operator Values  
Human‑readable descriptions.  
Tools should treat these as labels, not logic.

---

# 6. Micro‑Echo Schema

Maps each macro dimension to its micro‑echo (0.1–0.9).

```json
"microEchoes": {
  "1D": 0.1,
  "2D": 0.2,
  "3D": 0.3,
  "4D": 0.4,
  "5D": 0.5,
  "6D": 0.6,
  "7D": 0.7,
  "8D": 0.8,
  "9D": 0.9
}
```

Constraints:

- Keys must match `"1D"` through `"9D"`  
- Values must be floats between `0.1` and `0.9`  
- 0D intentionally has **no** micro‑echo  

---

# 7. Full Example

```json
{
  "0D": {
    "domain": "Quantum",
    "triad": ["Potential", "Fluctuation", "Emergence"]
  },
  "1D": {
    "domain": "BeingX",
    "triad": ["Presence", "Instantiation", "Persistence"]
  },
  "2D": {
    "domain": "BeingY",
    "triad": ["Self", "Other", "Boundary"]
  },
  "3D": {
    "domain": "BeingZ",
    "triad": ["Substance", "Shape", "Coherence"]
  },
  "4D": {
    "domain": "KnowingX",
    "triad": ["Sensation", "Attention", "Registration"]
  },
  "5D": {
    "domain": "KnowingY",
    "triad": ["Pattern", "Meaning-Making", "Contextualization"]
  },
  "6D": {
    "domain": "KnowingZ",
    "triad": ["Synthesis", "Relation", "Model-Formation"]
  },
  "7D": {
    "domain": "MeaningX",
    "triad": ["Relevance", "Valence", "Orientation"]
  },
  "8D": {
    "domain": "MeaningY",
    "triad": ["Aim", "Intention", "Directionality"]
  },
  "9D": {
    "domain": "MeaningZ",
    "triad": ["Coherence", "Completion", "Significance"]
  },

  "operators": {
    "OmegaB": "Being → Knowing",
    "PsiK": "Knowing → Meaning",
    "LambdaM": "Meaning → Being"
  },

  "microEchoes": {
    "1D": 0.1,
    "2D": 0.2,
    "3D": 0.3,
    "4D": 0.4,
    "5D": 0.5,
    "6D": 0.6,
    "7D": 0.7,
    "8D": 0.8,
    "9D": 0.9
  }
}
```

---

# 8. Versioning

Add a version field if you want future compatibility:

```json
"version": "1.0.0"
```

Recommended semantic versioning:

- **MAJOR** — breaking schema changes  
- **MINOR** — new fields, new dimensions, new operators  
- **PATCH** — typo fixes, metadata updates  

---

# 9. Extension Rules

To keep the ecosystem stable:

### Allowed extensions:
- Add new fields under each dimension (e.g., `"description"`, `"examples"`)  
- Add new operators (e.g., `"PhiC": "Cognition → Creativity"`)  
- Add new metadata sections  

### Not allowed:
- Changing the meaning of existing keys  
- Removing required fields (`domain`, `triad`)  
- Renaming dimensions (`"1D"`, `"2D"`, etc.)  

---

# 10. Recommended Tooling

Tools should:

- treat unknown fields as optional  
- treat missing fields as errors  
- preserve ordering of dimensions  
- avoid hard‑coding triad names

# 🧪 1. **Schema Validator (JS + Python versions)**

## **A. JavaScript Validator (Node)**  
Save as: `tools/validate-thought.js`

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";

const file = process.argv[2] || "docs/data/thought-ladder.json";
const raw = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8");
const data = JSON.parse(raw);

function fail(msg) {
  console.error("❌ Schema Error:", msg);
  process.exit(1);
}

function checkTriad(dim, entry) {
  if (!entry.domain) fail(`${dim}: missing 'domain'`);
  if (!Array.isArray(entry.triad)) fail(`${dim}: 'triad' must be an array`);
  if (entry.triad.length !== 3) fail(`${dim}: triad must have exactly 3 elements`);
  entry.triad.forEach((t, i) => {
    if (typeof t !== "string") fail(`${dim}: triad[${i}] must be a string`);
  });
}

const dims = Object.keys(data).filter(k => k.endsWith("D"));
dims.sort((a, b) => parseInt(a) - parseInt(b));

dims.forEach(dim => checkTriad(dim, data[dim]));

if (!data.operators) fail("Missing 'operators'");
["OmegaB", "PsiK", "LambdaM"].forEach(op => {
  if (!data.operators[op]) fail(`Missing operator '${op}'`);
});

if (!data.microEchoes) fail("Missing 'microEchoes'");
for (let i = 1; i <= 9; i++) {
  const key = `${i}D`;
  const val = data.microEchoes[key];
  if (typeof val !== "number") fail(`microEchoes.${key} must be a number`);
}

console.log("✅ Thought‑Ladder JSON is valid.");
```

Run:

```
node tools/validate-thought.js
```

---

## **B. Python Validator**  
Save as: `tools/validate_thought.py`

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json, sys

path = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "docs/data/thought-ladder.json"

def fail(msg):
    print("❌ Schema Error:", msg)
    sys.exit(1)

with open(path) as f:
    data = json.load(f)

dims = sorted([k for k in data if k.endswith("D")], key=lambda x: int(x[:-1]))

for dim in dims:
    entry = data[dim]
    if "domain" not in entry:
        fail(f"{dim}: missing 'domain'")
    if "triad" not in entry:
        fail(f"{dim}: missing 'triad'")
    triad = entry["triad"]
    if not isinstance(triad, list) or len(triad) != 3:
        fail(f"{dim}: triad must be a list of 3 strings")
    if not all(isinstance(t, str) for t in triad):
        fail(f"{dim}: triad elements must be strings")

ops = data.get("operators")
if not ops:
    fail("Missing 'operators'")
for op in ["OmegaB", "PsiK", "LambdaM"]:
    if op not in ops:
        fail(f"Missing operator '{op}'")

echo = data.get("microEchoes")
if not echo:
    fail("Missing 'microEchoes'")
for i in range(1, 10):
    key = f"{i}D"
    if key not in echo:
        fail(f"Missing microEcho for {key}")
    if not isinstance(echo[key], (int, float)):
        fail(f"microEchoes.{key} must be numeric")

print("✅ Thought‑Ladder JSON is valid.")
```

# 📐 2. **JSON‑Schema File (Automated Validation)**  
Save as: `docs/data/thought-ladder.schema.json`

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "title": "Thought Ladder Schema",
  "type": "object",

  "properties": {
    "0D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "1D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "2D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "3D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "4D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "5D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "6D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "7D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "8D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },
    "9D": { "$ref": "#/$defs/dimension" },

    "operators": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["OmegaB", "PsiK", "LambdaM"],
      "properties": {
        "OmegaB": { "type": "string" },
        "PsiK": { "type": "string" },
        "LambdaM": { "type": "string" }
      }
    },

    "microEchoes": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["1D", "2D", "3D", "4D", "5D", "6D", "7D", "8D", "9D"],
      "properties": {
        "1D": { "type": "number" },
        "2D": { "type": "number" },
        "3D": { "type": "number" },
        "4D": { "type": "number" },
        "5D": { "type": "number" },
        "6D": { "type": "number" },
        "7D": { "type": "number" },
        "8D": { "type": "number" },
        "9D": { "type": "number" }
      }
    }
  },

  "required": ["0D","1D","2D","3D","4D","5D","6D","7D","8D","9D","operators","microEchoes"],

  "$defs": {
    "dimension": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["domain", "triad"],
      "properties": {
        "domain": { "type": "string" },
        "triad": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": { "type": "string" },
          "minItems": 3,
          "maxItems": 3
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
```

This works with any JSON‑Schema validator.
